`PermittivityData`) store complex values as `(real, imag)` tuples for JSON stability.\n94\t\n95\t## Field Monitor Facts (task-037)\n96\t\n97\t- `src/autofdtd/compiler/monitors.py` now provides `CompiledFieldMonitor`, `FieldMonitorState`, `compile_field_monitor()` for field monitor compilation onto resolved grids.\n98\t- `CompiledFieldMonitor` carries monitor metadata (name, type, center, size, fields, interval, start) plus discrete placements and weights derived from the resolved grid.\n99\t- `FieldMonitorState` is a frozen dataclass that stores runtime recording state; time-domain monitors use `time_series` dict and `time_stamps` list, while frequency-domain monitors use `dft_data` dict and `dft_count` counter.\n100\t- Frozen dataclass field updates use `object.__setattr__` and `_set_dft_count` / `dft_count_value` accessors to bypass immutability for accumulating state.\n101\t- `src/autofdtd/kernels/monitors.py` provides `record_field_time_domain()`, `record_field_frequency_domain()`, `sample_field_at_points()`, and `accumulate_flux()` for field recording kernels.\n102\t- `src/autofdtd/runtime/monitors.py` provides `build_field_monitor_runtime()`, `record_monitor_fields()`, and `extract_monitor_data()` for runtime field monitor orchestration.\n103\t- `monitor_to_ir()` is exported from `autofdtd.ir` for IR lowering of all monitor types.\n104\t- Field monitor compilation uses `_compile_monitor_axis_placement()` which derives placements from monitor center/size against grid boundaries.\n105\t- FieldMonitor with explicit `freqs` tuple is treated as frequency-domain (`is_frequency_domain=True`), otherwise it is not (even if monitor_type is FieldMonitor).\n106\t- Time-domain monitors record at every `interval` steps starting from `start` step; `record_monitor_fields()` handles the step filtering.\n107\t- `tests/test_monitors_field.py` has 23 tests covering compilation, state initialization, time-domain recording, frequency-domain recording, flux accumulation, field sampling, and IR lowering.\n108\t\n109\t## Flux Monitor Facts (task-039)\n110\t\n111\t- `src/autofdtd/compiler/monitors.py` now provides `CompiledFluxMonitor`, `FluxMonitorState`, `compile_flux_monitor()` for flux monitor compilation onto resolved grids.\n112\t- `_infer_flux_normal_axis()` derives the normal axis from monitor size (the dimension with size=0), raising ValueError if no dimension is zero.\n113\t- `CompiledFluxMonitor` carries monitor metadata (name, type, center, size, normal_axis, direction, placements, interval, start) plus discrete surface placements and weights.\n114\t- `FluxMonitorState` is a frozen dataclass for flux recording; time-domain uses `flux_series` list and `time_stamps` list, frequency-domain uses `dft_flux` array and `dft_count` counter.\n115\t- `FluxMonitorState.record_flux_time_domain()` and `accumulate_flux_dft()` compute Poynting vector flux through the surface using `accumulate_flux()` kernel.\n116\t- `accumulate_flux()` computes the surface integral of the Poynting vector S = E × H; for axis=0 (x-normal): S_x = E_y*H_z - E_z*H_y; axis=1 (y-normal): S_y = E_z*H_x - E_x*H_z; axis=2 (z-normal): S_z = E_x*H_y - E_y*H_x.\n117\t- `FluxData` stores flux values; for time-domain (FluxTimeMonitor) `flux` is `tuple[float, ...]`, for frequency-domain (FluxMonitor with freqs) `flux` is `tuple[tuple[float, float], ...]` as (real, imag) complex tuples.\n118\t- `src/autofdtd/runtime/monitors.py` provides `build_flux_monitor_runtime()`, `record_monitor_flux()`, and `extract_flux_monitor_data()` for runtime flux monitor orchestration.\n119\t- `src/autofdtd/kernels/monitors.py` provides `flux_monitor_kernel_metadata()`, `record_flux_time_domain()`, and `record_flux_frequency_domain()` for flux recording kernels.\n120\t- `FluxMonitor` with `freqs` tuple is frequency-domain; `FluxTimeMonitor` is always time-domain.\n121\t- `tests/test_monitors_flux.py` has 39 tests covering normal axis inference, model construction, compilation, time-domain and frequency-domain recording, flux integration (Poynting vector), IR lowering, and energy-flow validation.\n122\t\n123\t## Decisions\n124\t\n125\t- Reserve subsystem namespaces now: `api`, `core`, `geometry`, `materials`, `boundaries`, `sources`, `monitors`, `grid`, `modes`, `ir`, `compiler`, `runtime`, `kernels`, `diagnostics`, `examples`.\n126\t- Use `hatchling` with a `src/` layout and ship a small CLI (`autofdtd-feature-matrix`) so the scaffold has a concrete install/runtime smoke target before solver code exists.\n127\t- Keep this task focused on packaging, docs, and planning metadata; full tagged models remain for `task-002` and `task-003`.\n128\t- Model ordered overlap explicitly through `Scene.structures_in_resolution_order()`, sorting by resolved priority and then insertion order so later structures still win ties during sequential materialization.\n129\t- Keep the initial container layer serialization-light: `to_payload()`, `to_json_text()`, and `write_json()` are enough for snapshots and handoff, while `task-005` can generalize the shared model base.\n130\t- Use a generic family-tagged component envelope in the IR so Phase 1 can package realistic simulations before every feature family has concrete typed IR classes; later tasks can specialize payloads without changing bundle shape.\n131\t- Bundle packaging is directory-based: `manifest.json` points at a hashed `simulation.json` entrypoint, avoiding self-referential manifest hashing while staying suitable for remote upload or local caching.\n132\t- Core validation is checklist-driven: implemented feature tags pass, deferred or rejected tags fail early with explicit errors, and known primitive geometry payloads validate only when enough fields are present so placeholder tagged dicts can survive until their concrete family tasks land.\n133\t- Shared model behavior should live in `autofdtd.core.models`; public API and IR classes should subclass it rather than duplicating serialization or copy helpers in subsystem-local base classes.\n134\t- Primitive geometry stays axis-aligned in Phase 1 public models; scene-compilation transforms are still explicit so later `Transformed` and grouped-geometry tasks can lower onto the same contract instead of rewriting primitive handling.\n135\t- Structure precedence is now inspectable with `Scene.resolve_structure_at_point()`, which mirrors materialization order by applying primitive containment checks in resolved-priority order.\n136\t- Keep `GeometryArray` as a public wrapper only; repeated placements lower into ordinary grouped and transformed instances so runtime/compiler code can treat arrays as pre-expanded ordered geometry.\n137\t- Phase 1 `PolySlab` scope is intentionally limited to constant-cross-section extrusions of simple polygons on principal axes; non-zero `dilation` or `sidewall_angle`, self-intersections, and curved or boolean polygon features must fail explicitly until later geometry tasks land.\n138\t- Keep the feature ledger conservative for clipping: `ClipOperation` remains a deferred family in `planning.py`, but geometry validation explicitly allows the implemented subset and rejects unsupported boolean ops with clear errors.\n139\t- Phase 1 transform wrappers are rigid only; free-form affine matrices and exact boolean meshing remain out of scope even though the public model now supports grouped and transformed composition.\n140\t- Keep the top-level simulation grid surface strict: `grid_spec` must be a `GridSpec`, `AutoGrid` requires `GridSpec.wavelength`, and `QuasiUniformGrid` remains an explicit deferred error.\n141\t- Phase 1 AutoGrid is deliberately narrow: derive a vacuum baseline from wavelength and domain size, discretize piecewise with a simple `max_scale` grading pass, and limit refinement inputs to `LayerRefinementSpec` body and bound hints rather than full geometry-driven meshing.\n142\t- Keep Phase 1 subpixel support narrow and explicit: dielectric interfaces may use `PolarizedAveraging` or `Staircasing`, while metal, PEC, PMC, and lossy-metal interfaces stay staircasing-only until anisotropic media and conformal policies land.\n143\t- Treat PEC and PMC as explicit constitutive clamp modes in coefficient compilation and runtime helpers instead of pretending they are extremely lossy ordinary media.\n144\t- Phase 1 `PoleResidue` keeps complex numbers JSON-safe as `(real, imag)` tuples in the public API and IR so serialization stays stable without inventing ad hoc string encoding.\n145\t- Phase 1 PoleResidue runtime support is electric-only and uses one complex polarization accumulator per pole; magnetic dispersion remains out of scope until later material families require it.\n146\t- Phase 1 `Sellmeier` support is intentionally passive-only and homogeneous-only: `B_i >= 0`, `C_i >= 0`, zero-`C_i` terms fold into `eps_inf`, and all timestep execution reuses the existing electric-only `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` path instead of creating a new auxiliary-state layout.\n147\t- Phase 1 `Lorentz` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `damping >= 0`, `frequency^2 != damping^2`), `Drude` is limited to damped poles (`damping > 0`) so it can lower cleanly onto the current pole-residue path, and `Debye` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `tau > 0`).\n148\t- The current exact-hold pole-residue recurrence path can overflow for sufficiently stiff poles relative to `dt`; material compilation now raises an explicit error instead of emitting unusable coefficients, which is especially relevant for very small-`tau` Debye fits.\n149\t- Phase 1 anisotropy stops at diagonal tensor media: `AnisotropicMedium` is implemented and IR-lowered, `FullyAnisotropicMedium` remains an explicit deferred error for runtime compilation, and `Medium2D` remains a deferred direct-simulation feature with offline conversion helpers only.\n150\t- Advanced material policy is explicit rather than silent: `LossyMetalMedium` and perturbation media are parseable defer buckets with manual fallback helpers, while sampled `Custom*` media are parsed or bucketed only to produce clear reject errors.\n151\t- Phase 1 boundary runtime support now covers `Periodic`, `BlochBoundary`, `PECBoundary`, `PMCBoundary`, and baseline `PML`; `StablePML`, `Absorber`, and ABC-family entries remain outside the runtime compiler until later boundary tasks land.\n152\t- Conductor boundaries are represented as reflection-sign transforms rather than special-casing them inside the future timestep loop: PEC uses odd tangential electric and odd normal magnetic parity, while PMC uses the opposite parity assignment.\n153\t- Keep Phase 1 PML deliberately narrow: use Tidy3D-shaped `num_layers + parameters + extrude_structures` public models, compile one profile per boundary face, and treat the first runnable backend as a staged damping layer with persistent face memory rather than a full split-field CPML implementation.\n154\t- Keep `StablePML` as a preset-driven extension of the baseline staged PML backend in Phase 1 rather than a separate field-splitting formulation; runtime differentiation is through compiled profile metadata and defaults, not a distinct kernel family.\n155\t- Keep `Absorber` practical and explicit: Phase 1 uses a conductivity-ramp absorbing subset with no auxiliary memory contribution and a PEC-like outer-wall reflection policy, trading fidelity for a clear stable fallback when baseline PML is unsuitable.\n156\t- Keep `ABCBoundary` practical and narrow: Phase 1 implements only the explicit-parameter first-order subset, and automatic medium inference plus mode-based ABC stay deferred until scene-material sampling and mode-solver tasks are available.\n157\t- Keep runtime convergence explicit and narrow: Phase 1 shutoff is the Tidy3D-style ratio of instantaneous integrated electric-field intensity to its historical maximum, checked on a configurable step interval; material-aware CFL relaxation and richer convergence heuristics remain deferred.\n158\t- Keep runtime logging separate from benchmark metrics: Phase 1 emits deterministic progress records at step 0, configurable progress intervals, and final stop, while every shutoff check still lands in convergence evidence even when progress logging is downsampled.\n159\t- Keep Phase 1 source-time scope narrow and explicit: implement `GaussianPulse` and `ContinuousWave` as standalone immutable models first, preserve Tidy3D-style `freq0`/`fwidth`/`offset` semantics plus `remove_dc_component` on Gaussian pulses, and defer broader source-time families to their own tasks instead of hiding them behind loose dict payloads.\n160\t- Phase 1 `BroadbandPulse` is a clearly delimited analytic subset: represent it as a Gaussian-modulated carrier derived from `(freq_range, minimum_amplitude)` rather than trying to reproduce Tidy3D's licensed extension internals, and keep `CustomSourceTime` as explicit sample interpolation with nearest-value clamping outside the provided range.\n161\t- Phase 1 `UniformCurrentSource` uses primal-cell placement metadata rather than hidden source snapping: zero-size axes interpolate between neighboring cell centers when enabled, finite extents either select center-in-bounds cells or overlap-fraction weights when `confine_to_bounds=True`, and `\"total\"` amplitude normalization divides by the local transverse extent implied by the source geometry and resolved grid.\n162\t\n163\t## Task Handoffs\n164\t\n165\t- `task-003` should add versioned IR models under `autofdtd.ir` without coupling them to the public API module layout; it can treat `Simulation.to_payload()` as an interim handoff format, not the final IR contract.\n166\t- Later geometry, material, source, and monitor tasks can plug concrete tagged models into the existing container fields without changing the ordered-container semantics.\n167\t- `task-004` and `task-005` can reuse `ComponentIR.payload` plus the shared IR schema version constants instead of inventing another transport shape.\n168\t- Later geometry, source, monitor, boundary, and grid tasks should route new object models through `autofdtd.core.validation.normalize_component()` or extend its equivalents instead of scattering policy checks across each family.\n169\t- Later compiler work can rely on wrapper IR bounds as conservative planning envelopes; exact voxelization or boolean rasterization still belongs in scene compilation rather than the public geometry layer.\n170\t- Later compiler work can consume `StructureIR.source_index` plus `StructureIR.precedence_rank` directly when building ordered region materialization plans or conflict diagnostics.\n171\t- Later grid/compiler tasks should treat `LayerRefinementSpecIR` as planning metadata only; corner finding, snapping-point localization, and structure-derived meshing still need a separate scene-aware meshing pass.\n172\t- Material and compiler tasks should consume `SubpixelSpecIR.averaging_targets` and `requires_anisotropic_materialization` directly instead of re-deriving which interface families need tensor-valued effective media.\n173\t- Later runtime and chunk-planning tasks can treat `IsotropicMaterialCoefficients` plus `ConstitutiveMode` as the stable baseline constitutive contract before dispersive and anisotropic state is added.\n174\t- Later Lorentz, Drude, Debye, and Sellmeier tasks should lower onto the `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` auxiliary-state contract whenever the family can be reduced to pole-residue terms instead of creating parallel runtime state layouts.\n175\t- Later subpixel materialization work can target `AnisotropicMaterialCoefficients` plus the diagonal anisotropic kernel path instead of inventing a separate tensor-material runtime for effective-media smoothing.\n176\t- Later absorbing-boundary tasks should extend `CompiledBoundarySpec` and the boundary ghost-update stage rather than reworking the public `Boundary` or `BoundarySpec` container semantics; Bloch, symmetry, and baseline PML metadata now already live on that runtime contract.\n177\t- `task-022` should either preserve the `PMLParams` coefficient-shape contract for `StablePML` / `Absorber` or add a clearly separate compiled-face payload; `allocate_pml_boundary_state()` and `apply_boundary_stages()` are the current extension seams.\n178\t- Later ABC-boundary work should treat the current `pml_faces` metadata on `CompiledBoundarySpec` as the legacy absorbing-face alias and prefer the newer `stable_pml_faces` / `absorber_faces` tags when family-specific behavior matters.\n179\t- Later mode-ABC work should extend the new `abc_faces` / staged-ABC seam rather than folding mode-aware behavior into the generic halo transform path; the current deferral is specifically about mode-solver dependency, not about the ABC stage layout itself.\n180\t- Later execution-loop tasks should treat `CompiledRuntimeControls` and `RuntimeController` as the stable seam for stop reasons, early-stop evidence, and step-budget bookkeeping rather than burying convergence logic inside kernels or chunk schedulers.\n181\t- Later execution-loop tasks should route full Maxwell stepping through `RuntimeProgressLogger` / `RuntimeExecutionLog` instead of inventing a second progress format; benchmark tasks can layer throughput and JIT timing on top of the existing log bundle rather than mixing those concerns into debugging output.\n182\t- Later source-family tasks should reuse `source_time_model_from_value()` and `source_time_to_ir()` as the normalization and lowering seam for nested `source_time` fields instead of reimplementing waveform parsing inside each source model.\n183\t- Later source-time expansions should preserve the current JSON-safe complex-pair representation for custom envelopes so package serialization and IR transport stay stable even if dataset-backed APIs are added later.\n184\t- Later `PointDipole` and `CustomCurrentSource` work should reuse `CompiledAxisPlacement` / `CompiledUniformCurrentSource` conventions where possible, extending them for Yee-staggered component placement or dataset-driven amplitudes instead of inventing a separate source-placement runtime contract.\n185\t\n186\t## Chunk Contract Task Handoffs (task-047)\n187\t\n188\t- `phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md` defines the full chunk contract including `ChunkSpec`, `FaceHalo`, `ChunkLayout`, per-chunk PML/ABC state, and device/rank placement.\n189\t- task-048 (Warp timestepping kernel) should consume `ChunkLayout` and `ChunkSpec` to allocate per-chunk field arrays and implement chunk-local `electric_update_kernel` / `magnetic_update_kernel`.\n190\t- task-049 (runtime executor) should consume `CompiledSimulation` + `ChunkLayout` to build per-chunk field state and implement `run_chunked_simulation()` with stage order: boundary_exchange → electric_update → source_injection → boundary_exchange → magnetic_update → monitor_collection → pml_stage → convergence_check.\n191\t- task-050 (MPI halo exchange) should extend `ChunkLayout.rank_assignment` to multiple ranks and implement `mpi_halo_exchange()` using `exchange_plan`.\n192\t- task-051 (CFL / chunk balancing) should use `ChunkSpec.local_grid_shape` and per-axis `cell_sizes` to compute per-chunk `min_step`; use global minimum if varying.\n193\t- task-053 (monitor accumulation kernels) should use `ChunkSpec.monitor_indices` for owning-chunk routing and implement gather/reduce for multi-chunk monitors (flux surfaces, mode overlaps).\n194\t- `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec` (`compiler/boundaries.py`) is the concrete refactoring point — replace with per-face `FaceHalo` from `ChunkLayout`.\n195\t\n196\t## Mode Solver Facts (task-031)\n197\t\n198\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/__init__.py` now exports the public mode-solver API including `ModeSpec`, `ModeSolverConfig`, `ModeSolverCrossSection`, `BoundaryCondition`, `ModeSolution`, `solve_modes`, `assemble_mode_matrix`, `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d`, and full IR lowering.\n199\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/models.py` provides `ModeSpec`, `ModeSolverCrossSection` (with normal_axis and boundary tuple), `BoundaryCondition` (dirichlet/neumann/bloch), and `ModeSolution` with field components stored as `(real, imag)` tuples for JSON stability.\n200\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/solver.py` implements `solve_modes` using a 2-component curl-curl FDTD eigenvalue formulation: `A·[Hx;Hy] = β²·[Hx;Hy]` with sparse matrix assembly and scipy `eigs` for the generalized eigenvalue problem. The formulation uses the VectorModesolver.jl paper (IEEE) as the reference for coefficient derivations.\n201\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/epsilon.py` provides `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d` which samples a Scene's permittivity onto a rectilinear cross-sectional grid, returning an `EpsilonCallback` compatible with the mode solver.\n202\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/ir.py` provides typed IR models `ModeSpecIR`, `BoundaryConditionIR`, `ModeSolverCrossSectionIR`, `ModeSolverConfigIR`, `ModeSolutionIR` with lowering functions `mode_spec_to_ir`, `boundary_condition_to_ir`, `cross_section_to_ir`, `mode_solver_config_to_ir`, `mode_solution_to_ir`.\n203\t- The solver requires scipy and will skip (not fail) when scipy is unavailable. The assembly and solve use double precision.\n204\t- Phase 1 mode solver scope: works with general anisotropic epsilon tensors, supports Dirichlet/Neumann/Bloch BCs, and produces ModeSolution objects with field components and power normalization.\n205\t- Known limitations: the current curl-curl formulation produces neff ≈ n_clad for weakly guided modes in small domains due to discretization scaling (1/h² vs k² ratios); the formulation needs careful physical parameter selection for truly guided-mode separation. This is a known FDTD eigenvalue challenge and is acceptable for Phase 1 as the subsystem interface and architecture are Warp-ready.\n206\t\n207\t## Monitor Task Handoffs (task-036)\n208\t\n209\t- Later monitor tasks (task-037 for field monitors, task-038 for medium monitors, task-039 for flux monitors, etc.) should reuse `monitor_to_ir()` for IR lowering and `monitor_model_from_value()` for validation/normalization instead of reimplementing these patterns.\n210\t- Later monitor tasks should extend `SimulationData` with specific registration methods (like `register_field_monitor`) and use `FieldData`, `FluxData`, `ModeData` etc. as the data container types.\n211\t- Monitor IR lowering is designed to be extensible: `monitor_to_ir()` falls back to generic `ComponentIR` for unknown types, so later tasks can add new monitor IR types without modifying the lowering contract.\n212\t- The `SimulationData.monitor_store` field is excluded from serialization by design - runtime monitor data is populated during simulation execution, not serialized with the simulation metadata.\n213\t- `Phase1_MONITOR_TYPES` in `core/validation.py` and `_MONITOR_CONSTRUCTORS` in `monitors/models.py` are the canonical lists of implemented monitor types; add new types to both when implementing new monitor families.\n214\t\n215\t## Task Handoffs (continued)\n216\t\n217\t## Medium Monitor Facts public API surfaces.\n243\t- All five projection monitors are added to `planning.py` feature matrix as IMPLEMENT features.\n244\t- `src/autofdtd/monitors/models.py` now provides data types accumulation.\n254\t\n255\t## Monitor Accumulation Kernel Facts (task-053)\n256\t\n257\t- `src/autofdtd/runtime/monitors.py` provides complete monitor runtime spines for all Phase 1 monitor types:\n258\t  - `build_field_monitor_runtime()` / `record_monitor_fields()` / `extract_monitor_data()` for field monitors\n259\t  - `build_flux_monitor_runtime()` / `record_monitor_flux()` / `extract_flux_monitor_data()` for flux monitors\n260\t  - `build_medium_monitor_runtime()` / `extract_medium_monitor_data()` for medium monitors\n261\t  - `build_mode_monitor_runtime()` / `record_monitor_modes()` / `extract_mode_monitor_data()` for mode monitors\n262\t  - `build_projection_monitor_runtime()` / `record_projection_monitor_dft()` / `extract_projection_monitor_data()` for projection monitors\n263\t  - `build_surface_field_monitor_runtime()` / `record_surface_monitor_fields()` / `extract_surface_field_monitor_data()` for surface field monitors\n264\t- `src/autofdtd/runtime/__init__.py` exports all monitor runtime functions; missing exports added in this task.\n265\t- DFT accumulation bug fixed: `dft_data` shape must be `(n_pts, n_freqs)` not `(n_pts,)`. Broadcasting in `accumulate_dft` uses `values[:, None] * phase[None, :]` for proper (n_pts, n_freqs) result.\n266\t- `src/autofdtd/compiler/monitors.py` fixed `FieldMonitorState` and `SurfaceFieldMonitorState` `__post_init__` to allocate `(n_pts, n_freqs)` DFT arrays.\n267\t- `accumulate_dft` simplified to always use `values[:, None] * phase[None, :]` (no conditional branching).\n268\t- All monitor data types use JSON-safe `(real, imag)` tuple format for complex values.\n269\t- `tests/test_monitors_accumulation.py` provides 33 integration tests covering compile → build_runtime → record → extract for all monitor types.\n270\t\n271\t## Scene-Compilation Pipeline Facts (task-045)\n272\t\n273\t- `src/autofdtd/compiler/pipeline.py` now provides the top-level `compile_simulation()` entry point that orchestrates the full compilation pipeline from public API → IR → compiled runtime artifacts.\n274\t- `CompiledSimulation` is a frozen dataclass (not Pydantic compilation, (4) scene materialization, (5) source planning, (6) monitor planning, (7) boundary planning.\n283\t\n284\t## Chunk Architecture Facts (task-047)\n285\t\n286\t- Chunk is the architectural unit for field storage, field update, and parallelism; rank or device is placement, not computation.\n287\t- Current Phase 1 architecture is **monolithic**: `CompiledSimulation` treats the entire grid as one chunk with global `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec`.\n288\t- `ChunkSpec` (in `phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md`) is the planning metadata for one chunk: `chunk_index`, `global_bounds`, `interior_bounds`, `owned_bounds`, `local_grid_shape`, `face_halos`, `source_indices`, `monitor_indices`.\n289\t- `FaceHalo` describes per-face halo metadata: `depth`, `neighbor_chunk_index`, `exchange_kind`, `phase_factor`, `electric_signs`, `magnetic_signs`, `pml_profile`, `abc_coefficients`.\n290\t- `ChunkLayout` is the global decomposition plan: `num_chunks`, `chunks`, `device_assignment` (chunk_index → CUDA device), `rank_assignment` (chunk_index → MPI rank), `exchange_plan`.\n291\t- Halo exchange is **stage-specific** and **component-family-specific**: `boundary_exchange` before E update, `boundary_exchange` before H update, `pml_stage` after H update.\n292\t- Halo depth is **per-face**, not global: periodic/Bloch=1 cell, PEC/PMC=1 cell, PML=variable (num_layers), ABC=1 cell, symmetry=1 cell, interior=1 cell.\n293\t- Symmetry reduction produces `ChunkSpec.is_reduced` and `ChunkSpec.symmetry_multiplicity`; symmetry transform is a logical address transform, not a data-layout change.\n294\t- Periodic/Bloch remapping: `ChunkLayout.exchange_plan` connects plus face of last chunk to minus face of first chunk; Bloch exchange includes `phase_factor` from `BlochBoundaryIR`.\n295\t- PML/ABC state must be **per-chunk per-face**: `ChunkPMLFaceState` (sigma/kappa/alpha profiles + memory array) and `ChunkABCFaceState` (previous_boundary, previous_adjacent arrays).\n296\t- `ChunkSpec.source_indices` and `ChunkSpec.monitor_indices` route source injection and monitor recording to owning chunks.\n297\t- For CUDA: field arrays are `wp.array` objects per chunk on target device; Warp kernel launches use `chunk.local_grid_shape` as launch dimensions.\n298\t- `halo_depth=1` global constant in `CompiledBoundarySpec` must be replaced with per-face `FaceHalo.depth` — this is a concrete refactoring point.\n299\t- Key deferred items: MPI halo exchange (task-050+), dynamic load balancing (Meep-style chunk_balancer), overlapped/systolic execution (task-052+), chunk-local CFL.\n300\t\n301\t## Warp Backend Conventions Facts (task-048)\n302\t\n303\t- `src/autofdtd/kernels/backend.py` now provides the foundational Warp backend conventions: device management, persistent array tags, module stability tracking, complex field policy, and profiling hooks.\n304\t- `src/autofdtd/kernels/steps.py` now provides the core = force_complex or has_bloch_axis`. Bloch boundaries force complex fields even when `bloch_vec == 0`.\n308\t- `ModuleStability` tracks stable modules: `mark_module_stable(name)` and `is_module_stable(name)` prevent JIT churn from unstable kernels.\n309\t- Capture-safe stepping: all timestep-varying values (dt, time, step_index) are passed as explicit kernel arguments; no closure captures in kernel definitions.\n310\t- Profiling hooks: `WarpTimer(name)` context manager (GPU-side timing via ScopedTimer when available, CPU fallback otherwise), `nvtx_range(name, color)` for NVTX annotation, `step_metrics()` for structured Gcells/s metrics.\n311\t- `step_kernel_metadata()` returns update stage order: `boundary_exchange → electric_update → source_injection → boundary_exchange → magnetic_update → pml_stage → monitor_collection → convergence_check`.\n312\t- `curl_component_mapping` documents the Yee lattice curl formula for each field component: Ex uses (Hz, dy, Hy, dz), etc.\n313\t- `tests/test_kernels_backend.py` has 27 tests covering availability detection, device management, array tags, allocation helpers, complex field policy, module stability, and profiling hooks.\n314\t- Warp is not installed in the current environment; all allocation helpers fall back to NumPy when Warp is unavailable.\n315\t\n316\t## Source Injection Scheduling Facts (task-052)\n317\t\n318\t- `SurfaceFieldMonitorIR`, `SurfaceFieldTimeMonitorIR`.\n- `monitor_to_ir()` function in `ir/models.py` lowers concrete monitor models to typed IR payloads, falling back to generic `ComponentIR` for unknown or incomplete monitor types.\n- `SimulationIR.monitors` field is typed as `tuple[MonitorComponentIR, ...]` where `MonitorComponentIR` is the union of all typed monitor IRs plus `ComponentIR`.\n- `src/autofdtd/core/validation.py` now has `_normalize_monitor()` and `_PHASE1_MONITOR_TYPES` frozenset for monitor validation; `normalize_component()` dispatches to `_normalize_monitor()` for `context=\"monitor\"`.\n- `src/autofdtd/planning.py` now has `ModeSpec` and `MediumMonitor` added to the feature matrix as IMPLEMENT features since they are used by `ModeMonitor` and `ModeSolverMonitor`.\n- Monitor data types (`FieldData`, `FluxData`, `ModeData`, `PermittivityData`) store complex values as `(real, imag)` tuples for JSON stability.\n\n## Field Monitor Facts (task-037)\n\n- `src/autofdtd/compiler/monitors.py` now provides `CompiledFieldMonitor`, `FieldMonitorState`, `compile_field_monitor()` for field monitor compilation onto resolved grids.\n- `CompiledFieldMonitor` carries monitor metadata (name, type, center, size, fields, interval, start) plus discrete placements and weights derived from the resolved grid.\n- `FieldMonitorState` is a frozen dataclass that stores runtime recording state; time-domain monitors use `time_series` dict and `time_stamps` list, while frequency-domain monitors use `dft_data` dict and `dft_count` counter.\n- Frozen dataclass field updates use `object.__setattr__` and `_set_dft_count` / `dft_count_value` accessors to bypass immutability for accumulating state.\n- `src/autofdtd/kernels/monitors.py` provides `record_field_time_domain()`, `record_field_frequency_domain()`, `sample_field_at_points()`, and `accumulate_flux()` for field recording kernels.\n- `src/autofdtd/runtime/monitors.py` provides `build_field_monitor_runtime()`, `record_monitor_fields()`, and `extract_monitor_data()` for runtime field monitor orchestration.\n- `monitor_to_ir()` is exported from `autofdtd.ir` for IR lowering of all monitor types.\n- Field monitor compilation uses `_compile_monitor_axis_placement()` which derives placements from monitor center/size against grid boundaries.\n- FieldMonitor with explicit `freqs` tuple is treated as frequency-domain (`is_frequency_domain=True`), otherwise it is not (even if monitor_type is FieldMonitor).\n- Time-domain monitors record at every `interval` steps starting from `start` step; `record_monitor_fields()` handles the step filtering.\n- `tests/test_monitors_field.py` has 23 tests covering compilation, state initialization, time-domain recording, frequency-domain recording, flux accumulation, field sampling, and IR lowering.\n\n## Flux Monitor Facts (task-039)\n\n- `src/autofdtd/compiler/monitors.py` now provides `CompiledFluxMonitor`, `FluxMonitorState`, `compile_flux_monitor()` for flux monitor compilation onto resolved grids.\n- `_infer_flux_normal_axis()` derives the normal axis from monitor size (the dimension with size=0), raising ValueError if no dimension is zero.\n- `CompiledFluxMonitor` carries monitor metadata (name, type, center, size, normal_axis, direction, placements, interval, start) plus discrete surface placements and weights.\n- `FluxMonitorState` is a frozen dataclass for flux recording; time-domain uses `flux_series` list and `time_stamps` list, frequency-domain uses `dft_flux` array and `dft_count` counter.\n- `FluxMonitorState.record_flux_time_domain()` and `accumulate_flux_dft()` compute Poynting vector flux through the surface using `accumulate_flux()` kernel.\n- `accumulate_flux()` computes the surface integral of the Poynting vector S = E × H; for axis=0 (x-normal): S_x = E_y*H_z - E_z*H_y; axis=1 (y-normal): S_y = E_z*H_x - E_x*H_z; axis=2 (z-normal): S_z = E_x*H_y - E_y*H_x.\n- `FluxData` stores flux values; for time-domain (FluxTimeMonitor) `flux` is `tuple[float, ...]`, for frequency-domain (FluxMonitor with freqs) `flux` is `tuple[tuple[float, float], ...]` as (real, imag) complex tuples.\n- `src/autofdtd/runtime/monitors.py` provides `build_flux_monitor_runtime()`, `record_monitor_flux()`, and `extract_flux_monitor_data()` for runtime flux monitor orchestration.\n- `src/autofdtd/kernels/monitors.py` provides `flux_monitor_kernel_metadata()`, `record_flux_time_domain()`, and `record_flux_frequency_domain()` for flux recording kernels.\n- `FluxMonitor` with `freqs` tuple is frequency-domain; `FluxTimeMonitor` is always time-domain.\n- `tests/test_monitors_flux.py` has 39 tests covering normal axis inference, model construction, compilation, time-domain and frequency-domain recording, flux integration (Poynting vector), IR lowering, and energy-flow validation.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- Reserve subsystem namespaces now: `api`, `core`, `geometry`, `materials`, `boundaries`, `sources`, `monitors`, `grid`, `modes`, `ir`, `compiler`, `runtime`, `kernels`, `diagnostics`, `examples`.\n- Use `hatchling` with a `src/` layout and ship a small CLI (`autofdtd-feature-matrix`) so the scaffold has a concrete install/runtime smoke target before solver code exists.\n- Keep this task focused on packaging, docs, and planning metadata; full tagged models remain for `task-002` and `task-003`.\n- Model ordered overlap explicitly through `Scene.structures_in_resolution_order()`, sorting by resolved priority and then insertion order so later structures still win ties during sequential materialization.\n- Keep the initial container layer serialization-light: `to_payload()`, `to_json_text()`, and `write_json()` are enough for snapshots and handoff, while `task-005` can generalize the shared model base.\n- Use a generic family-tagged component envelope in the IR so Phase 1 can package realistic simulations before every feature family has concrete typed IR classes; later tasks can specialize payloads without changing bundle shape.\n- Bundle packaging is directory-based: `manifest.json` points at a hashed `simulation.json` entrypoint, avoiding self-referential manifest hashing while staying suitable for remote upload or local caching.\n- Core validation is checklist-driven: implemented feature tags pass, deferred or rejected tags fail early with explicit errors, and known primitive geometry payloads validate only when enough fields are present so placeholder tagged dicts can survive until their concrete family tasks land.\n- Shared model behavior should live in `autofdtd.core.models`; public API and IR classes should subclass it rather than duplicating serialization or copy helpers in subsystem-local base classes.\n- Primitive geometry stays axis-aligned in Phase 1 public models; scene-compilation transforms are still explicit so later `Transformed` and grouped-geometry tasks can lower onto the same contract instead of rewriting primitive handling.\n- Structure precedence is now inspectable with `Scene.resolve_structure_at_point()`, which mirrors materialization order by applying primitive containment checks in resolved-priority order.\n- Keep `GeometryArray` as a public wrapper only; repeated placements lower into ordinary grouped and transformed instances so runtime/compiler code can treat arrays as pre-expanded ordered geometry.\n- Phase 1 `PolySlab` scope is intentionally limited to constant-cross-section extrusions of simple polygons on principal axes; non-zero `dilation` or `sidewall_angle`, self-intersections, and curved or boolean polygon features must fail explicitly until later geometry tasks land.\n- Keep the feature ledger conservative for clipping: `ClipOperation` remains a deferred family in `planning.py`, but geometry validation explicitly allows the implemented subset and rejects unsupported boolean ops with clear errors.\n- Phase 1 transform wrappers are rigid only; free-form affine matrices and exact boolean meshing remain out of scope even though the public model now supports grouped and transformed composition.\n- Keep the top-level simulation grid surface strict: `grid_spec` must be a `GridSpec`, `AutoGrid` requires `GridSpec.wavelength`, and `QuasiUniformGrid` remains an explicit deferred error.\n- Phase 1 AutoGrid is deliberately narrow: derive a vacuum baseline from wavelength and domain size, discretize piecewise with a simple `max_scale` grading pass, and limit refinement inputs to `LayerRefinementSpec` body and bound hints rather than full geometry-driven meshing.\n- Keep Phase 1 subpixel support narrow and explicit: dielectric interfaces may use `PolarizedAveraging` or `Staircasing`, while metal, PEC, PMC, and lossy-metal interfaces stay staircasing-only until anisotropic media and conformal policies land.\n- Treat PEC and PMC as explicit constitutive clamp modes in coefficient compilation and runtime helpers instead of pretending they are extremely lossy ordinary media.\n- Phase 1 `PoleResidue` keeps complex numbers JSON-safe as `(real, imag)` tuples in the public API and IR so serialization stays stable without inventing ad hoc string encoding.\n- Phase 1 PoleResidue runtime support is electric-only and uses one complex polarization accumulator per pole; magnetic dispersion remains out of scope until later material families require it.\n- Phase 1 `Sellmeier` support is intentionally passive-only and homogeneous-only: `B_i >= 0`, `C_i >= 0`, zero-`C_i` terms fold into `eps_inf`, and all timestep execution reuses the existing electric-only `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` path instead of creating a new auxiliary-state layout.\n- Phase 1 `Lorentz` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `damping >= 0`, `frequency^2 != damping^2`), `Drude` is limited to damped poles (`damping > 0`) so it can lower cleanly onto the current pole-residue path, and `Debye` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `tau > 0`).\n- The current exact-hold pole-residue recurrence path can overflow for sufficiently stiff poles relative to `dt`; material compilation now raises an explicit error instead of emitting unusable coefficients, which is especially relevant for very small-`tau` Debye fits.\n- Phase 1 anisotropy stops at diagonal tensor media: `AnisotropicMedium` is implemented and IR-lowered, `FullyAnisotropicMedium` remains an explicit deferred error for runtime compilation, and `Medium2D` remains a deferred direct-simulation feature with offline conversion helpers only.\n- Advanced material policy is explicit rather than silent: `LossyMetalMedium` and perturbation media are parseable defer buckets with manual fallback helpers, while sampled `Custom*` media are parsed or bucketed only to produce clear reject errors.\n- Phase 1 boundary runtime support now covers `Periodic`, `BlochBoundary`, `PECBoundary`, `PMCBoundary`, and baseline `PML`; `StablePML`, `Absorber`, and ABC-family entries remain outside the runtime compiler until later boundary tasks land.\n- Conductor boundaries are represented as reflection-sign transforms rather than special-casing them inside the future timestep loop: PEC uses odd tangential electric and odd normal magnetic parity, while PMC uses the opposite parity assignment.\n- Keep Phase 1 PML deliberately narrow: use Tidy3D-shaped `num_layers + parameters + extrude_structures` public models, compile one profile per boundary face, and treat the first runnable backend as a staged damping layer with persistent face memory rather than a full split-field CPML implementation.\n- Keep `StablePML` as a preset-driven extension of the baseline staged PML backend in Phase 1 rather than a separate field-splitting formulation; runtime differentiation is through compiled profile metadata and defaults, not a distinct kernel family.\n- Keep `Absorber` practical and explicit: Phase 1 uses a conductivity-ramp absorbing subset with no auxiliary memory contribution and a PEC-like outer-wall reflection policy, trading fidelity for a clear stable fallback when baseline PML is unsuitable.\n- Keep `ABCBoundary` practical and narrow: Phase 1 implements only the explicit-parameter first-order subset, and automatic medium inference plus mode-based ABC stay deferred until scene-material sampling and mode-solver tasks are available.\n- Keep runtime convergence explicit and narrow: Phase 1 shutoff is the Tidy3D-style ratio of instantaneous integrated electric-field intensity to its historical maximum, checked on a configurable step interval; material-aware CFL relaxation and richer convergence heuristics remain deferred.\n- Keep runtime logging separate from benchmark metrics: Phase 1 emits deterministic progress records at step 0, configurable progress intervals, and final stop, while every shutoff check still lands in convergence evidence even when progress logging is downsampled.\n- Keep Phase 1 source-time scope narrow and explicit: implement `GaussianPulse` and `ContinuousWave` as standalone immutable models first, preserve Tidy3D-style `freq0`/`fwidth`/`offset` semantics plus `remove_dc_component` on Gaussian pulses, and defer broader source-time families to their own tasks instead of hiding them behind loose dict payloads.\n- Phase 1 `BroadbandPulse` is a clearly delimited analytic subset: represent it as a Gaussian-modulated carrier derived from `(freq_range, minimum_amplitude)` rather than trying to reproduce Tidy3D's licensed extension internals, and keep `CustomSourceTime` as explicit sample interpolation with nearest-value clamping outside the provided range.\n- Phase 1 `UniformCurrentSource` uses primal-cell placement metadata rather than hidden source snapping: zero-size axes interpolate between neighboring cell centers when enabled, finite extents either select center-in-bounds cells or overlap-fraction weights when `confine_to_bounds=True`, and `\"total\"` amplitude normalization divides by the local transverse extent implied by the source geometry and resolved grid.\n\n## Task Handoffs\n\n- `task-003` should add versioned IR models under `autofdtd.ir` without coupling them to the public API module layout; it can treat `Simulation.to_payload()` as an interim handoff format, not the final IR contract.\n- Later geometry, material, source, and monitor tasks can plug concrete tagged models into the existing container fields without changing the ordered-container semantics.\n- `task-004` and `task-005` can reuse `ComponentIR.payload` plus the shared IR schema version constants instead of inventing another transport shape.\n- Later geometry, source, monitor, boundary, and grid tasks should route new object models through `autofdtd.core.validation.normalize_component()` or extend its equivalents instead of scattering policy checks across each family.\n- Later compiler work can rely on wrapper IR bounds as conservative planning envelopes; exact voxelization or boolean rasterization still belongs in scene compilation rather than the public geometry layer.\n- Later compiler work can consume `StructureIR.source_index` plus `StructureIR.precedence_rank` directly when building ordered region materialization plans or conflict diagnostics.\n- Later grid/compiler tasks should treat `LayerRefinementSpecIR` as planning metadata only; corner finding, snapping-point localization, and structure-derived meshing still need a separate scene-aware meshing pass.\n- Material and compiler tasks should consume `SubpixelSpecIR.averaging_targets` and `requires_anisotropic_materialization` directly instead of re-deriving which interface families need tensor-valued effective media.\n- Later runtime and chunk-planning tasks can treat `IsotropicMaterialCoefficients` plus `ConstitutiveMode` as the stable baseline constitutive contract before dispersive and anisotropic state is added.\n- Later Lorentz, Drude, Debye, and Sellmeier tasks should lower onto the `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` auxiliary-state contract whenever the family can be reduced to pole-residue terms instead of creating parallel runtime state layouts.\n- Later subpixel materialization work can target `AnisotropicMaterialCoefficients` plus the diagonal anisotropic kernel path instead of inventing a separate tensor-material runtime for effective-media smoothing.\n- Later absorbing-boundary tasks should extend `CompiledBoundarySpec` and the boundary ghost-update stage rather than reworking the public `Boundary` or `BoundarySpec` container semantics; Bloch, symmetry, and baseline PML metadata now already live on that runtime contract.\n- `task-022` should either preserve the `PMLParams` coefficient-shape contract for `StablePML` / `Absorber` or add a clearly separate compiled-face payload; `allocate_pml_boundary_state()` and `apply_boundary_stages()` are the current extension seams.\n- Later ABC-boundary work should treat the current `pml_faces` metadata on `CompiledBoundarySpec` as the legacy absorbing-face alias and prefer the newer `stable_pml_faces` / `absorber_faces` tags when family-specific behavior matters.\n- Later mode-ABC work should extend the new `abc_faces` / staged-ABC seam rather than folding mode-aware behavior into the generic halo transform path; the current deferral is specifically about mode-solver dependency, not about the ABC stage layout itself.\n- Later execution-loop tasks should treat `CompiledRuntimeControls` and `RuntimeController` as the stable seam for stop reasons, early-stop evidence, and step-budget bookkeeping rather than burying convergence logic inside kernels or chunk schedulers.\n- Later execution-loop tasks should route full Maxwell stepping through `RuntimeProgressLogger` / `RuntimeExecutionLog` instead of inventing a second progress format; benchmark tasks can layer throughput and JIT timing on top of the existing log bundle rather than mixing those concerns into debugging output.\n- Later source-family tasks should reuse `source_time_model_from_value()` and `source_time_to_ir()` as the normalization and lowering seam for nested `source_time` fields instead of reimplementing waveform parsing inside each source model.\n- Later source-time expansions should preserve the current JSON-safe complex-pair representation for custom envelopes so package serialization and IR transport stay stable even if dataset-backed APIs are added later.\n- Later `PointDipole` and `CustomCurrentSource` work should reuse `CompiledAxisPlacement` / `CompiledUniformCurrentSource` conventions where possible, extending them for Yee-staggered component placement or dataset-driven amplitudes instead of inventing a separate source-placement runtime contract.\n\n## Chunk Contract Task Handoffs (task-047)\n\n- `phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md` defines the full chunk contract including `ChunkSpec`, `FaceHalo`, `ChunkLayout`, per-chunk PML/ABC state, and device/rank placement.\n- task-048 (Warp timestepping kernel) should consume `ChunkLayout` and `ChunkSpec` to allocate per-chunk field arrays and implement chunk-local `electric_update_kernel` / `magnetic_update_kernel`.\n- task-049 (runtime executor) should consume `CompiledSimulation` + `ChunkLayout` to build per-chunk field state and implement `run_chunked_simulation()` with stage order: boundary_exchange → electric_update → source_injection → boundary_exchange → magnetic_update → monitor_collection → pml_stage → convergence_check.\n- task-050 (MPI halo exchange) should extend `ChunkLayout.rank_assignment` to multiple ranks and implement `mpi_halo_exchange()` using `exchange_plan`.\n- task-051 (CFL / chunk balancing) should use `ChunkSpec.local_grid_shape` and per-axis `cell_sizes` to compute per-chunk `min_step`; use global minimum if varying.\n- task-053 (monitor accumulation kernels) should use `ChunkSpec.monitor_indices` for owning-chunk routing and implement gather/reduce for multi-chunk monitors (flux surfaces, mode overlaps).\n- `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec` (`compiler/boundaries.py`) is the concrete refactoring point — replace with per-face `FaceHalo` from `ChunkLayout`.\n\n## Mode Solver Facts (task-031)\n\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/__init__.py` now exports the public mode-solver API including `ModeSpec`, `ModeSolverConfig`, `ModeSolverCrossSection`, `BoundaryCondition`, `ModeSolution`, `solve_modes`, `assemble_mode_matrix`, `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d`, and full IR lowering.\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/models.py` provides `ModeSpec`, `ModeSolverCrossSection` (with normal_axis and boundary tuple), `BoundaryCondition` (dirichlet/neumann/bloch), and `ModeSolution` with field components stored as `(real, imag)` tuples for JSON stability.\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/solver.py` implements `solve_modes` using a 2-component curl-curl FDTD eigenvalue formulation: `A·[Hx;Hy] = β²·[Hx;Hy]` with sparse matrix assembly and scipy `eigs` for the generalized eigenvalue problem. The formulation uses the VectorModesolver.jl paper (IEEE) as the reference for coefficient derivations.\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/epsilon.py` provides `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d` which samples a Scene's permittivity onto a rectilinear cross-sectional grid, returning an `EpsilonCallback` compatible with the mode solver.\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/ir.py` provides typed IR models `ModeSpecIR`, `BoundaryConditionIR`, `ModeSolverCrossSectionIR`, `ModeSolverConfigIR`, `ModeSolutionIR` with lowering functions `mode_spec_to_ir`, `boundary_condition_to_ir`, `cross_section_to_ir`, `mode_solver_config_to_ir`, `mode_solution_to_ir`.\n- The solver requires scipy and will skip (not fail) when scipy is unavailable. The assembly and solve use double precision.\n- Phase 1 mode solver scope: works with general anisotropic epsilon tensors, supports Dirichlet/Neumann/Bloch BCs, and produces ModeSolution objects with field components and power normalization.\n- Known limitations: the current curl-curl formulation produces neff ≈ n_clad for weakly guided modes in small domains due to discretization scaling (1/h² vs k² ratios); the formulation needs careful physical parameter selection for truly guided-mode separation. This is a known FDTD eigenvalue challenge and is acceptable for Phase 1 as the subsystem interface and architecture are Warp-ready.\n\n## Monitor Task Handoffs (task-036)\n\n- Later monitor tasks (task-037 for field monitors, task-038 for medium monitors, task-039 for flux monitors, etc.) should reuse `monitor_to_ir()` for IR lowering and `monitor_model_from_value()` for validation/normalization instead of reimplementing these patterns.\n- Later monitor tasks should extend `SimulationData` with specific registration methods (like `register_field_monitor`) and use `FieldData`, `FluxData`, `ModeData` etc. as the data container types.\n- Monitor IR lowering is designed to be extensible: `monitor_to_ir()` falls back to generic `ComponentIR` for unknown types, so later tasks can add new monitor IR types without modifying the lowering contract.\n- The `SimulationData.monitor_store` field is excluded from serialization by design - runtime monitor data is populated during simulation execution, not serialized with the simulation metadata.\n- `Phase1_MONITOR_TYPES` in `core/validation.py` and `_MONITOR_CONSTRUCTORS` in `monitors/models.py` are the canonical lists of implemented monitor types; add new types to both when implementing new monitor families.\n\n## Task Handoffs (continued)\n\n## Medium Monitor Facts (task-038)\n\n- `MediumMonitorData` model in `monitors/models.py` stores diagonal components (eps_xx, eps_yy, eps_zz, mu_xx, mu_yy, mu_zz) as `(real, imag)` tuples for JSON stability.\n- `PermittivityMonitor` already had `PermittivityData` with real-valued eps components; `MediumMonitorData` extends with complex-valued permittivity + permeability.\n- `SimulationData` now has `register_medium_monitor()` and `register_permittivity_monitor()` methods for data registration.\n- `CompiledMediumMonitor` in `compiler/monitors.py` compiles medium/permittivity monitors onto resolved grids using the same axis-placement logic as field monitors.\n- `MediumMonitorState` allocates a `(n_pts, num_freqs, n_components)` array at init: 6 components for MediumMonitor (eps_xx, eps_yy, eps_zz, mu_xx, mu_yy, mu_zz) or 3 for PermittivityMonitor.\n- `MediumMonitorState.populate_from_scene()` can sample scene media at monitor placements and compute complex permittivity/permeability at each frequency.\n\n## GaussianOverlapMonitor Facts (task-041)\n\n- `GaussianOverlapMonitor` now exposes beam parameters (`angle_theta`, `angle_phi`, `pol_angle`, `waist_radius`, `waist_distance`) matching Tidy3D's IMPLEMENT features.\n- Phase 1 runtime/compiler support for overlap computation is deferred to future task-053 (monitor accumulation kernel stages) since the monitors have the full API surface, IR lowering, and data types in place.\n- `SimulationData` now has `register_gaussian_overlap_monitor()` and `register_astigmatic_gaussian_overlap_monitor()` methods for data registration.\n- returns `MediumMonitorData` or `PermittivityData` depending on the monitor type.\n- Phase 1 medium monitors sample static material properties (not dynamic field-dependent), so recording is a one-time scene sampling rather than per-timestep accumulation.\n- IR lowering for MediumMonitor and PermittivityMonitor was already implemented in task-036 via `monitor_to_ir()`.\n## Projection Monitor Facts simplified Rayleigh approximation (equivalence principle with surface J = n × H and M = -n × E); full Green's function postprocessing is deferred to task-057.\n- `monitor_to_ir()` in `ir/models.py` already lowered all five projection monitor types to typed IR via the existing `monitor_to_ir()` infrastructure.\n- `tests/test_monitors_projection.py` has 24 tests covering model construction, data types, SimulationData registration, IR lowering, compilation, and DFT accumulation.\n\n## Monitor Accumulation Kernel Facts (task-053)\n\n- `src/autofdtd/runtime/monitors.py` provides complete monitor runtime spines for all Phase 1 monitor types:\n  - `build_field_monitor_runtime()` / `record_monitor_fields()` / `extract_monitor_data()` for field monitors\n  - `build_flux_monitor_runtime()` / `record_monitor_flux()` / `extract_flux_monitor_data()` for flux monitors\n  - `build_medium_monitor_runtime()` / `extract_medium_monitor_data()` for medium monitors\n  - `build_mode_monitor_runtime()` / `record_monitor_modes()` / `extract_mode_monitor_data()` for mode monitors\n  - `build_projection_monitor_runtime()` / `record_projection_monitor_dft()` / `extract_projection_monitor_data()` for projection monitors\n  - `build_surface_field_monitor_runtime()` / `record_surface_monitor_fields()` / `extract_surface_field_monitor_data()` for surface field monitors\n- `src/autofdtd/runtime/__init__.py` exports all monitor runtime functions; missing exports added in this task.\n- DFT accumulation bug fixed: `dft_data` shape must be `(n_pts, n_freqs)` not `(n_pts,)`. Broadcasting in `accumulate_dft` uses `values[:, None] * phase[None, :]` for proper (n_pts, n_freqs) result.\n- `src/autofdtd/compiler/monitors.py` fixed `FieldMonitorState` and `SurfaceFieldMonitorState` `__post_init__` to allocate `(n_pts, n_freqs)` DFT arrays.\n- `accumulate_dft` simplified to always use `values[:, None] * phase[None, :]` (no conditional branching).\n- All monitor data types use JSON-safe `(real, imag)` tuple format for complex values.\n- `tests/test_monitors_accumulation.py` provides 33 integration tests covering compile → build_runtime → record → extract for all monitor types.\n\n## Scene-Compilation Pipeline Facts (task-045)\n\n- `src/autofdtd/compiler/pipeline.py` now provides the top-level `compile_simulation()` entry point that orchestrates the full compilation pipeline from public API → IR → compiled runtime artifacts.\n- `CompiledSimulation` is a frozen dataclass (not Pydantic model) that holds all compiled artifacts: `simulation_ir`, `resolved_grid`, `runtime_controls`, `scene_coefficients`, `compiled_sources`, `compiled_monitors`, `compiled_boundaries`.\n- `CompiledSceneCoefficients` holds compiled background medium and per-structure material coefficients plus scene material samples for diagnostics.\n- `CompiledSources` is a container for all source types: `uniform_current`, `point_dipole`, `custom_current`, `custom_field`, `plane_wave`, `gaussian_beam`, `astigmatic_gaussian_beam`, `mode_source`, `tfsf`.\n- `CompiledMonitors` is a container for all monitor types: `field`, `flux`, `medium`, `mode`, `projection`.\n- `CompiledBoundaries` holds `CompiledBoundarySpec` plus flags for PML/ABC state needs.\n- `compile_simulation()` uses runtime controls `dt` for material coefficient compilation since dispersive media need the timestep for recurrence coefficients.\n- `_compile_scene_coefficients()` samples grid cell centers for diagnostics but the actual material field allocation is deferred to the geometry discretization task (task-046).\n- `simulation_to_execution_package()` combines compilation with `ExecutionPackageIR` bundling for transportable simulation bundles.\n- Pipeline stages: (1) grid resolution, (2) IR lowering via `simulation_to_ir()`, (3) runtime controls compilation, (4) scene materialization, (5) source planning, (6) monitor planning, (7) boundary planning.\n\n## Chunk Architecture Facts (task-047)\n\n- Chunk is the architectural unit for field storage, field update, and parallelism; rank or device is placement, not computation.\n- Current Phase 1 architecture is **monolithic**: `CompiledSimulation` treats the entire grid as one chunk with global `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec`.\n- `ChunkSpec` (in `phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md`) is the planning metadata for one chunk: `chunk_index`, `global_bounds`, `interior_bounds`, `owned_bounds`, `local_grid_shape`, `face_halos`, `source_indices`, `monitor_indices`.\n- `FaceHalo` describes per-face halo metadata: `depth`, `neighbor_chunk_index`, `exchange_kind`, `phase_factor`, `electric_signs`, `magnetic_signs`, `pml_profile`, `abc_coefficients`.\n- `ChunkLayout` is the global decomposition plan: `num_chunks`, `chunks`, `device_assignment` (chunk_index → CUDA device), `rank_assignment` (chunk_index → MPI rank), `exchange_plan`.\n- Halo exchange is **stage-specific** and **component-family-specific**: `boundary_exchange` before E update, `boundary_exchange` before H update, `pml_stage` after H update.\n- Halo depth is **per-face**, not global: periodic/Bloch=1 cell, PEC/PMC=1 cell, PML=variable (num_layers), ABC=1 cell, symmetry=1 cell, interior=1 cell.\n- Symmetry reduction produces `ChunkSpec.is_reduced` and `ChunkSpec.symmetry_multiplicity`; symmetry transform is a logical address transform, not a data-layout change.\n- Periodic/Bloch remapping: `ChunkLayout.exchange_plan` connects plus face of last chunk to minus face of first chunk; Bloch exchange includes `phase_factor` from `BlochBoundaryIR`.\n- PML/ABC state must be **per-chunk per-face**: `ChunkPMLFaceState` (sigma/kappa/alpha profiles + memory array) and `ChunkABCFaceState` (previous_boundary, previous_adjacent arrays).\n- `ChunkSpec.source_indices` and `ChunkSpec.monitor_indices` route source injection and monitor recording to owning chunks.\n- For CUDA: field arrays are `wp.array` objects per chunk on target device; Warp kernel launches use `chunk.local_grid_shape` as launch dimensions.\n- `halo_depth=1` global constant in `CompiledBoundarySpec` must be replaced with per-face `FaceHalo.depth` — this is a concrete refactoring point.\n- Key deferred items: MPI halo exchange (task-050+), dynamic load balancing (Meep-style chunk_balancer), overlapped/systolic execution (task-052+), chunk-local CFL.\n\n## Warp Backend Conventions Facts (task-048)\n\n- `src/autofdtd/kernels/backend.py` now provides the foundational Warp backend conventions: device management, persistent array tags, module stability tracking, complex field policy, and profiling hooks.\n- `src/autofdtd/kernels/steps.py` now provides the core Maxwell timestepping kernel definitions: `electric_update_3d`, `magnetic_update_3d`, `MaxwellStepKernelSpec`, `allocate_maxwell_arrays()`, `step_maxwell()`, and `step_kernel_metadata()`.\n- `WARP_AVAILABLE = warp is not None` is the canonical availability flag; each kernel module has its own `WARP_AVAILABLE` derived from the optional `import warp as wp`.\n- `allocate_field_array()` allocates `(Nx, Ny, Nz, 3)` vector fields with `FieldArrayTag` attached; `allocate_coefficient_array()` allocates `(Nx, Ny, Nz)` coefficient fields with `CoefficientArrayTag`; `allocate_auxiliary_array()` allocates `(num_poles, Nx, Ny, Nz)` polarization arrays with `AuxiliaryArrayTag`.\n- `ComplexFieldPolicy` determines when complex dtype is required: `requires_complex = force_complex or has_bloch_axis`. Bloch boundaries force complex fields even when `bloch_vec == 0`.\n- `ModuleStability` tracks stable modules: `mark_module_stable(name)` and `is_module_stable(name)` prevent JIT churn from unstable kernels.\n- Capture-safe stepping: all timestep-varying values (dt, time, step_index) are passed as explicit kernel arguments; no closure captures in kernel definitions.\n- Profiling hooks: `WarpTimer(name)` context manager (GPU-side timing via ScopedTimer when available, CPU fallback otherwise), `nvtx_range(name, color)` for NVTX annotation, `step_metrics()` for structured Gcells/s metrics.\n- `step_kernel_metadata()` returns update stage order: `boundary_exchange → electric_update → source_injection → boundary_exchange → magnetic_update → pml_stage → monitor_collection → convergence_check`.\n- `curl_component_mapping` documents the Yee lattice curl formula for each field component: Ex uses (Hz, dy, Hy, dz), etc.\n- `tests/test_kernels_backend.py` has 27 tests covering availability detection, device management, array tags, allocation helpers, complex field policy, module stability, and profiling hooks.\n- Warp is not installed in the current environment; all allocation helpers fall back to NumPy when Warp is unavailable.\n\n## Source Injection Scheduling Facts (task-052)\n\n- `src/autofdtd/runtime/sources.py` now provides `apply_source_injection_stage()` as the unified runtime entry point for all source types in a single call.\n- `src/autofdtd/kernels/sources.py` now provides `inject_sources_stage()` as the kernel-level unified source injection function.\n- Both functions apply sources in the following order: uniform_current → point_dipole → custom_current → custom_field → mode_source → plane_wave → gaussian_beam → astigmatic_gaussian_beam → tfsf.\n- Scheduling semantics follow `step_kernel_metadata()`: `source_injection` stage runs after `electric_update` and before `magnetic_update`: `boundary_exchange → electric_update → source_injection → boundary_exchange → magnetic_update → pml_stage`.\n- Both functions accept `time`, `dt`, and `freq` parameters for time-varying and frequency-dependent sources.\n- `tests/test_sources_scheduling.py` has 13 tests covering: no-source case, individual source types (uniform current, plane wave, TFSF), multiple source types, kernel/runtime equivalence, time evolution, field shape preservation, freq parameter, point dipole, custom current/field sources, and field accumulation.\n- All 87 source-related tests pass.\n\n## Chunk-Aware Runtime Facts (task-054)\n\n- `src/autofdtd/ir/models.py` now has typed chunk IR models: `FaceHaloIR`, `ChunkSpecIR`, `ExchangeDescriptorIR`, `ChunkLayoutIR` for transportable chunk decomposition metadata.\n- `chunk_layout_to_ir()`, `_chunk_spec_to_ir()`, `_face_halo_to_ir()`, `_exchange_descriptor_to_ir()` provide IR lowering for chunk metadata.\n- `src/autofdtd/runtime/boundaries.py` now provides chunk-aware halo exchange helpers:\n  - `pack_halo()` packs halo cells from one face of a chunk's field array\n  - `unpack_halo()` unpacks halo cells into one face of a chunk's field array\n  - `apply_signs_to_halo()` applies PEC/PMC/symmetry reflection signs to halo data\n  - `apply_phase_to_halo()` applies Bloch phase factor to halo data\n  - `ChunkHaloExchange` class manages halo exchange operations for a chunk layout\n  - `build_chunk_halo_exchange()` builds a chunk-aware halo exchange manager\n- `src/autofdtd/compiler/pipeline.py` `CompiledSimulation` now includes `chunk_layout: ChunkLayout` field.\n- `compile_simulation()` now builds `ChunkLayout` via `build_chunk_layout(num_chunks=(1,1,1), grid_shape, cell_sizes, boundary_spec, symmetry)` and includes it in `CompiledSimulation`.\n- `src/autofdtd/runtime/__init__.py` exports the new halo exchange functions.\n- `tests/test_kernels_boundaries.py` now has 14 new tests for halo exchange helpers, `ChunkHaloExchange`, and chunk IR lowering (44 total tests pass).\n- Phase 1 chunk layout is **monolithic**: single chunk with `num_chunks=(1,1,1)`, full domain coverage, per-face PML halos with depth from `num_layers`.\n- `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec` is preserved for backward compatibility with existing boundary kernel paths; chunk-aware code uses `FaceHalo.depth` from `ChunkLayout` instead.\n\n","numLines":344,"startLine":1,"totalLines":344}}}

pipeline: public API → IR → compiled runtime artifacts.\n\nThis module provides the top-level compilation entry point that orchestrates the\nfull pipeline from validated public API objects (Simulation, Scene, Structure)\ninto the compiled runtime artifacts needed for execution.\n\nPipeline Stages\n---------------\n\n1. **Grid Resolution**\n   - Resolve ``grid_spec`` against simulation domain (center, size)\n   - Produces ``ResolvedGrid`` with per-axis cell boundaries and step sizes\n\n2. **IR Lowering**\n   - Lower ``Simulation`` → ``SimulationIR`` via ``simulation_to_ir()``\n   - Scene structures inherit precedence metadata from ``structure_precedence()``\n   - Sources, monitors, boundaries, subpixel policy lower to typed IR payloads\n\n3. **Scene Materialization**\n   - Compile scene background medium coefficients\n   - Compile each structure's medium into coefficient fields\n   - Allocate material auxiliary state descriptors (PoleResidue, Anisotropic)\n\n4. **Source Planning**\n   - Compile each source onto the resolved grid\n   - Produce discrete placements, weights, and amplitude metadata\n\n5. **Monitor Planning**\n   - Compile each monitor onto the resolved grid\n   - Produce discrete placements and recording metadata\n\n6. **Boundary Planning**\n   - Compile boundary spec into per-face metadata\n   - Allocate PML/ABC boundary state descriptors where needed\n\n7. **Runtime Controls**\n   - Compile stop policy (run_time, num_time_steps, shutoff)\n   - Produce ``CompiledRuntimeControls`` for the stepping loop\n\nOutputs\n-------\n\n``CompiledSimulation`` holds all artifacts needed for runtime execution:\n\n- ``simulation_ir``: The tagged IR snapshot (for transport/inspection)\n- ``resolved_grid``: The discretized simulation grid\n- ``runtime_controls``: Compiled stop-policy metadata\n- ``scene_coefficients``: Material coefficient fields and metadata\n- ``compiled_sources``: Per-source placement and amplitude metadata\n- ``compiled_monitors``: Per-monitor placement and recording metadata\n- ``compiled_boundaries``: Per-face boundary metadata and state descriptors\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import \"\"\"Compile scene materials into coefficient fields.\"\"\"\n\n    # Compile scene background medium\n    background = compile_medium_coefficients(scene.medium, dt=dt)\n\n    # Compile structure None = None,\n    shutoff_check_interval: int = 10,\n) -> CompiledSimulation:\n    \"\"\"Compile a public Simulation into all runtime artifacts needed for execution.\n\n    This is the top-level entry point for the scene-compilation pipeline. It\n    orchestrates grid resolution, IR lowering, and compilation of materials,\n    sources, monitors, boundaries, and runtime controls.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    simulation : Simulation\n        The validated public simulation object to compile.\n    dt : float, optional\n        Override the compiled timestep size. Re-compiling the same simulation with the same parameters\n    produces equivalent artifacts.\n\n    Examples\n    --------\n    >>> from autofdtd import Simulation, Box, Medium, UniformGrid, compile_simulation\n    >>> sim = Simulation(\n    ...     size=(1.0, 1.0, 1.0),\n    ...     run_time=1e-12,\n    ...     grid_spec=UniformGrid(dl=0.01),\n    ... )\n    >>> compiled = compile_simulation(sim)\n    >>> compiled.grid_shape\n    (100, 100, 100)\n    >>> compiled.runtime_controls.num_time_steps  # doctest: +SKIP\n    1000\n    \"\"\"\n    # 1. Lower to IR\n    simulation_ir = simulation_to_ir(simulation)\n\n    # 3. Compile monitors\n    compiled_monitors = _compile_monitors_for_simulation(simulation, grid=resolved_grid)\n\n    # 7. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_function_huqk52n3vapn_2","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Core Maxwell timestepping kernels for Phase 1.\n2\t\n3\tThis module provides the GPU-accelerated E and H field update kernels.\n4\tThe kernels follow the Warp backend conventions documented in ``backend.py``.\n5\t\n6\tKernel Organization\n7\t------------------\n8\tThe Maxwell update is split into explicit stages:\n9\t\n10\t1. **boundary_exchange** — fill ghost cells before each update\n11\t2. **electric_update** — update electric field components\n12\t3. **source_injection** — add source contributions to E\n13\t4. **boundary_exchange** — re-exchange E ghosts before H update\n14\t5. **magnetic_update** — update magnetic field components\n15\t6. **pml_stage** — apply PML/ABC damping (post-H update)\n16\t7. **convergence_check** — integrated field intensity (Python-side)\n17\t\n18\tUpdate Equations\n19\t---------------\n20\tFor a Yee lattice with cell sizes ``(dx, dy, dz)`` and timestep ``dt``:\n21\t\n22\tElectric field update::\n23\t\n24\t    Ex[i+1] = Ex[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n25\t        (Hz[i,j+1,k] - Hz[i,j,k])/dy - (Hy[i,j,k+1] - Hy[i,j,k])/dz\n26\t    )\n27\t    Ey[i+1] = Ey[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n28\t        (Hx[i,j,k+1] - Hx[i,j,k])/dz - (Hz[i+1,j,k] - Hz[i,j,k])/dx\n29\t    )\n30\t    Ez[i+1] = Ez[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n31\t        (Hy[i+1,j,k] - Hy[i,j,k])/dx - (Hx[i,j+1,k] - Hx[i,j,k])/dy\n32\t    )\n33\t\n34\tMagnetic field update::\n35\t\n36\t    Hx[i+1] = Hx[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n37\t        (Ez[i,j+1,k] - Ez[i,j,k])/dy - (Ey[i,j,k+1] - Ey[i,j,k])/dz\n38\t    )\n39\t    Hy[i+1] = Hy[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n40\t        (Ex[i,j,k+1] - Ex[i,j,k])/dz - (Ez[i+1,j,k] - Ez[i,j,k])/dx\n41\t    )\n42\t    Hz[i+1] = Hz[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n43\t        (Ey[i+1,j,k] - Ey[i,j,k])/dx - (Ex[i,j+1,k] - Ex[i,j,k])/dy\n44\t    )\n45\t\n46\tCoefficients\n47\t-----------\n48\tFor efficiency, the coefficients are precomputed as::\n49\t\n50\t    ce_e = dt / (eps * eps0)    # for E update\n51\t    ce_h = -dt / (mu * mu0)     # for H update (note the sign)\n52\t    cm_e = dt / (eps * eps0)    # for magnetic current (same as ce_e)\n53\t    cm_h = -dt / (mu * mu0)     # for magnetic update (same as ce_h)\n54\t\n55\tIn a homogeneous medium these are constants. In an inhomogeneous medium\n56\tthey vary spatially and are sampled from coefficient arrays.\n57\t\n58\tCapture-Safe Stepping\n59\t---------------------\n60\tAll timestep-dependent values are passed as explicit kernel arguments:\n61\t\n62\t- ``dt``: scalar float timestep\n63\t- ``step_index``: int step counter (for time-varying sources)\n64\t- ``time``: float current simulation time\n65\t\n66\tSource amplitudes are computed in Python and passed as scalar or array\n67\targuments.
